# Book contradictions, omissions and resolutions

Source: independent review of the engineering book (`docs/book`), contracts, configuration and reference code by six
lens reviewers on 2026-10-22, each finding re-checked by an adversarial verifier (quotes and arithmetic re-done).
134 findings were confirmed; refuted findings are not listed. The book itself is preserved unchanged on
`docs/technical-book-v1`; resolutions are adopted through ADRs and code on the implementation branch.

Dispositions: `RESOLVED_IN_CODE` (cited code plus at least one test that exercises it), `RESOLVED_BY_ADR` (decided in
the cited ADR section), `ACCEPTED_LIMITATION` (stated limitation and where it is documented) or `OPEN` (what is
missing and the increment that owns it; increments are those of `docs/requirements/dependency-graph.md`). A
residual is a gap recorded inside an otherwise resolved finding. Tests marked (IBKR venv) run only where the
official `ibapi` client is installed and are skipped elsewhere. Passing offline tests is evidence of offline
behaviour only (ADR-0001).

Verified against `impl/foundation@73f7b03` on 2026-10-22; `impl/foundation@8b98d00` on 2026-10-22. Each disposition names the commit its substance was checked against; its line references are kept pointing at the current code, so they may be newer than that commit.

Disposition summary: RESOLVED_IN_CODE: 107, RESOLVED_BY_ADR: 6, ACCEPTED_LIMITATION: 4, OPEN: 17

| ID | Severity | Kind | Finding | Resolution (adopted, or reviewer proposal when untriaged) | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-DAT-01 | critical | contradiction | Chapter 20 lets automatic selection pick a merely 'validated' artifact, including in a running session; Chapter 10 and the status ladder require preapproval and owner action | Resolved for the research side. Artifacts have the statuses proposed, reproducible, validated, shadow, paper, eligible, active, quarantined and retired in a hash-chained transition log. validated -> shadow needs the owner's approval or the SYSTEM inside an owner-approved, immutable shadow envelope, and in shadow the strategy runner only records decisions; paper, eligible and active need a fresh owner approval per artifact; automatic selection runs as SYSTEM only under an owner-approved, immutable selection policy (the envelope) and considers only eligible, unexpired artifacts inside it. Point (3) holds in the core: a STRATEGY principal is an artifact id `<class>@<16 hex of the artifact hash>` bound to its token, acts only for sessions whose frozen mandate lists that id, and a new default never changes a running session's list. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:108-123`; `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:744-792`; `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:862-880`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:56-61`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:118-119`; `backend/src/unknown/domain/principal.py:62`; `docs/adr/0017-strategy-artifacts-promotion-and-runtime.md#Artifact identity` |
| BR-LED-01 | critical | ambiguity | Eq 9.1 leaves R and P undefined against the bucket list: on the Ch30 example its four readings give $104.10, $200, $4.10 or $100, and only the last is correct | Funding is bounded per owner, not by a residual formula. A seed moves only out of `unallocated`'s available cash (CASH + PAYABLE + FEES_ACCRUED minus active cash holds and net payments due on sales) less the operational buffer. Receivables are never available, protected proceeds are a separate owner, and ledger CASH is confirmed only when it equals broker settled cash. This is reading (d) of the finding: free cash = settled cash owned by unallocated. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/ledger.py:158-182`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/sessions.py:230-260`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:148-199`; `docs/adr/0006-ledger-owner-dimension.md#Holds (reservations)` |
| BR-OPS-01 | critical | ambiguity | Mac/AWS single-writer fencing has no authoritative ownership record, epoch or transfer protocol, and the book leaves automatic fenced replacement and manual transfer both open | ADR-0011: one execution_ownership record per account (deployment, monotonic epoch, status, grantor, provenance), acquired only by owner command. A paper or live hand-over needs the previous holder's release certificate (stop latched, no open orders, clean reconciliation) or an explicit owner attestation. Every order carries deployment:epoch:client-id, transmission requires HELD ownership, foreign-tagged or untagged activity latches the stop, and there is no automatic failover. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/admin.py:128-185`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:296-299`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:70-85`; `docs/adr/0011-execution-ownership-and-split-brain.md#Decision`; `docs/runbooks/README.md#RB-09` |
| BR-OPS-02 | critical | omission | A restore from backup silently rolls back the stop latch, command-ID dedup, outbox and successor state, enabling duplicate orders and unlatched trading | Restore detection (c41a505): a backup is marked inside the copy, and a database whose writer epoch is not above the <db>.epoch marker, or a non-empty one without it, is a restore. The process start then latches the stop (RESTORED_FROM_BACKUP), opens a blocking exception, turns in-flight and RESERVED orders into UNKNOWN with NEEDS_LOOKUP (never resent) and requires reconciliation before anything else. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/desk.py:342-391`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/admin.py:252-287`; `backend/src/unknown/store/db.py:194-206`; `backend/src/unknown/store/db.py:215-231`; `docs/runbooks/README.md#RB-10` |
| BR-OPS-03 | critical | omission | Owner identity and step-up authentication are never defined; R13 'signed policy' contradicts the unsigned schema, and agents on the Mac can act as the owner | Partly resolved by ADR-0013: owner identity is a passphrase (scrypt hash outside the repo) with HttpOnly session cookie, CSRF token, login backoff and step-up for start, policy, capital, ownership and exception commands; R13 is resolved as 'authority = authenticated owner command in the hash-chained log'. The core socket maps tokens to roles (API token: OWNER; agent token: AGENT; strategy tokens bound to one artifact), and the core re-checks the step-up age for start and broker-admin actions. Login, session and CSRF are now tested over HTTP for the plugin routes, and the terminal's Playwright suite exercises login, HTTP 428 step-up and the same-id retry against a real API. | OPEN; owner: I3 (owner authentication, ADR-0013) with I6 (agent OS isolation) | `docs/adr/0013-terminal-api-and-owner-authentication.md#Owner authentication`; `backend/src/unknown/api/auth.py`; `backend/src/unknown/api/server.py:58-66`; `backend/src/unknown/api/server.py:582-591`; `backend/src/unknown/service/core.py:55`; `backend/src/unknown/service/core.py:373-376`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/control.py:88-89` |
| BR-ORD-01 | critical | ambiguity | Outbox sender never says the attempt is persisted BEFORE transmission; a crash in that window resends a live order | Write-ahead (ADR-0009): outbox_begin commits SUBMIT_PENDING and the outbox row SENDING before the broker is called, and only PENDING rows are ever claimed, so a SENDING row is never sent again. On restart a SUBMIT_PENDING order or SENDING row becomes UNKNOWN with NEEDS_LOOKUP. The correlation id is the client order id in the order reference, and the IBKR adapter persists its orderId allocation before placeOrder and never reuses or resends it. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:285-345`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/admin.py:252-268`; `backend/src/unknown/gateway/runner.py:198-231`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/ibkr/ids.py` |
| BR-ORD-02 | critical | omission | Reservation can be released on terminal broker status before the executions that status reports are ingested | A terminal broker status whose filled quantity exceeds the ingested executions keeps the order's hold (broker_terminal_pending); the order turns terminal and releases only when ingested quantity reaches the broker's figure. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:165-173`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:460-470`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:88-91` |
| BR-ORD-03 | critical | omission | UNKNOWN has no exit rule: broker 'not found', retry identity and owner resolution are all unspecified | lookup() is tri-state (found / conclusively not found / cannot determine). A conclusive 'not found' for an order with no fills makes it REJECTED and releases it; 'found' applies the broker status; 'cannot determine' keeps the order UNKNOWN, reserved and blocking. Nothing is resubmitted: a retry is a new intent with a new order id. The owner closes a stuck UNKNOWN with resolve_exception action close_unknown_order, which needs fresh authentication, a note and broker evidence (BROKER_STATEMENT, BROKER_CONFIRM or SUPPORT_REFERENCE) and never releases a hold the broker may still work: the latest reconciliation must be complete, in this process, fresh and later than the order's last material change (a repeated Inactive report counts), must not list the order as open or have found it by lookup, and its only blocking differences may be those of unresolved UNKNOWN orders (a working order's difference refuses the close); otherwise CLOSE_NOT_PROVEN names every unmet condition and nothing changes. Only an UNKNOWN order is closed this way (ORDER_NOT_UNKNOWN). The order ends REJECTED (no fills) or CANCELED (fills kept), its holds are released, UnknownOrderClosedByOwner records the evidence, run and lookup, and the account must reconcile again (RB-13). | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/broker/port.py:138-145`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:92-125`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:347-355`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/control.py:253-338`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/control.py:343-356`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/ibkr/adapter.py:1121-1149`; `docs/runbooks/README.md#RB-13 Close a stuck UNKNOWN order on broker evidence` |
| BR-ORD-04 | critical | omission | Red stop does not cover reserved-but-unsent outbox rows or SUBMIT_PENDING/UNKNOWN orders | latch_stop cancels every open buy whatever its state: an untransmitted buy is canceled locally and its hold released; SUBMIT_PENDING, acknowledged, partially filled and UNKNOWN buys get an outbox CANCEL (by client order id, broker id optional) and keep their hold until the broker confirms. outbox_begin re-checks the entry gates inside its transaction, so a queued buy is never sent after a latch. Stop progress counts open buys, pending cancels and UNKNOWN orders. reqGlobalCancel is deliberately not used, because it would also cancel orders placed in TWS. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/control.py:28-73`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:242-265`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:316-334`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/port.py:235`; `docs/capabilities/register.json#reqGlobalCancel is an owner-confirmed emergency action only` |
| BR-ORD-05 | critical | contradiction | Deduplicating executions by provider_execution_id alone: no version field, so IBKR corrections double-count and same-id amendments collide | Related-id corrections are booked once: a higher IBKR revision of the same base reverses and rebooks the effective one (BR-LED-05). Decision for one execution id redelivered with changed economic content (ADR-0009 amendment): the booked original stands, nothing of the amendment is booked, the received payload is recorded in an ExecutionContentConflict event, and a blocking critical EXECUTION_ANOMALY naming the execution's order, session and current owner holds new exposure until the owner resolves it; a different amendment of the same id opens its own case, and an identical redelivery is a duplicate. Tested through the gateway runner for price and quantity amendments, repeats, resolution and reconciliation. A correction of a completed session's execution posts to protected:<id> with an owner case (BR-LED-03). | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:242-265`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/ibkr/mapping.py:166-176`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/ibkr/adapter.py:1646-1668`; `docs/adr/0009-order-lifecycle-and-outbox.md#Same execution id, changed content` |
| BR-ORD-06 | critical | contradiction | Broker read results carry no completeness, as-of time or scope, and nothing forbids inferring 'canceled' from absence | Every port read is typed and carries a completeness flag. A reconciliation with any incomplete read (snapshot, open orders, executions, or a non-converging execution re-read) is INCOMPLETE and only keeps entries blocked. A local order absent from a complete open-order list is looked up; only a conclusive 'never received' for an order with no fills makes it REJECTED, and an acknowledged order missing at the broker is a blocking difference, never inferred canceled. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/broker/port.py:106-113`; `backend/src/unknown/reconcile/reconciler.py:65-116`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:39-51`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:92-125` |
| BR-REF-03 | critical | reference-code-defect | After a restart, a risk-stopped system has no path to reconciliation, so it can never reduce positions; start is forbidden and no review command exists | Reconciliation is its own SYSTEM command and never touches the latch. A clean run in the new process clears the recovery gate, after which risk-reducing sells of unreserved session lots are accepted while the stop stays latched; an owner green start clears the latch. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:31-240`; `backend/src/unknown/risk/pretrade.py:94-99` |
| BR-REF-05 | critical | omission | The red stop never reaches the broker, and the contracts cannot express the cancels it needs | Stop enqueues an outbox CANCEL row per open buy, with its own outbox id keyed to the order, and the port cancels by client order id with the broker id optional, so SUBMIT_PENDING and UNKNOWN orders are covered. The sender re-checks the latch before every SUBMIT. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/control.py:28-46`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:242-282`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/port.py:235`; `backend/src/unknown/store/schema.py:118-122` |
| BR-REF-06 | critical | ambiguity | BrokerPort's lookup cannot say 'conclusively not received', and no call carries a fencing token, account scope or completeness fields | The port separates results: LookupResult is tri-state (found / conclusively not found / cannot determine), a cancel ACCEPTED is only an acknowledgement, and every read is typed with completeness. Fencing is local (ADR-0011) because IBKR enforces none; one adapter instance serves one account and the IBKR adapter latches if the host reports an unexpected account. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/broker/port.py:106-145`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:418-422`; `docs/adr/0011-execution-ownership-and-split-brain.md#Context` |
| BR-SES-01 | critical | error | Availability formula 9.1 has no session-allocation term, an undefined R, and a P that includes receivables, so it misstates fundable cash | Same resolution as BR-LED-01: each owner's availability is CASH + PAYABLE + FEES_ACCRUED minus holds and net payments due on sales, receivables are never available, protected proceeds are their own owner, and a seed is funded only from unallocated settled cash less the buffer, within the authorized-capital ceiling. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/ledger.py:158-182`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/sessions.py:230-260`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/admin.py:196-231`; `docs/adr/0006-ledger-owner-dimension.md#Holds (reservations)` |
| BR-SES-03 | critical | omission | Unsuccessful sessions have no terminal path, no rule for residual capital and no successor rule | ADR-0008: an unsuccessful session winds down, reconciles and ends CLOSED with an outcome (BELOW_TARGET, OWNER_ENDED, EXPIRED); all its cash and receivables return to `unallocated` (receivables stay non-available until settled); no successor is created; loss budgets are account-level and survive sessions, restarts and policy versions. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/sessions.py:365-420`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/sessions.py:478-485`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/valuation.py:107-144`; `docs/adr/0008-session-state-machine.md#Closing an unsuccessful session` |
| BR-DAT-02 | high | omission | A15 never defines which order states count as 'pending exposure', and decisions have no deterministic command identity, so a decision can be duplicated | Pending is defined once: the leaves of every order in a core open state (RESERVED, SUBMIT_PENDING, ACKNOWLEDGED, PARTIALLY_FILLED, CANCEL_PENDING, UNKNOWN), whoever placed it in the session. A pending buy counts as if it fills and a pending sell as already sold; terminal orders count nothing. The core's CONCENTRATION check adds the session's open buys in the instrument and GROSS_EXPOSURE every open buy of the account, each tested by a rejection against a control. The runner uses the same definition, emits no new intent for an instrument with an UNKNOWN or CANCEL_PENDING order in the session (PENDING_UNRESOLVED), allows at most one live intent per session, artifact and instrument (OPEN_INTENT), and checks both at the decision and again at transmission. Every command id is derived deterministically from (artifact, session date, instrument), so reprocessing a bar returns the original result. An UNKNOWN order also blocks new buys account-wide. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/order_machine.py:33`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:108-113`; `backend/src/unknown/risk/pretrade.py:153-157`; `backend/src/unknown/strategy/runner.py:252-269`; `backend/src/unknown/strategy/coreview.py:48-49`; `docs/strategy/README.md#Pending exposure (BR-DAT-02)` |
| BR-DAT-03 | high | error | Eq. 7.1 implementation shortfall: the reported execution component is wrong whenever an order partially fills | Resolved in the research engine: implementation_shortfall computes the corrected eq. 7.1, IS = s(sum q_j p_j - (sum q_j) p0) + fees + s(Q - sum q_j)(p_mark - p0), with execution, fee and opportunity components in exact decimals plus bps, the mark being the close of the execution session; every finished order records it. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/research/engine.py:397-410`; `backend/src/unknown/research/engine.py:595-596`; `backend/src/unknown/research/common.py:91` |
| BR-DAT-04 | high | error | Eq. 6.4 sizing leaves fixed and minimum fees out of the risk term; the worked example permits a trade whose loss at the stop is 3.5x its budget | research.sizing.plan_orders is the one A15 sizer, used by the backtest and the strategy runner. Its buy step limits the position to the largest whole Q with Q x (d + c) + Fee_entry + Fee_exit <= B_risk. Both fees come from risk.fees.envelope, the function the core reserves fees with (minimum per order included), under the active policy's fee model at runtime and the engine's schedule in the backtest; zero means no order. The cash term then sizes the buy to what the funding budget can reserve including the fee envelope. Spec schema v2 declares rules.sizing.risk_budget. The book's example sizes to 0 with a 0.75 minimum each way; against a real core, the 100.00 fixture session buys 1 share of a 10.00 instrument instead of 4, and none of a 42.00 one. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/research/sizing.py:131-157`; `backend/src/unknown/research/sizing.py:158-195`; `backend/src/unknown/research/spec.py:323-329`; `docs/strategy/README.md#Risk term (BR-DAT-04)` |
| BR-DAT-05 | high | omission | No rule for assigning availability time to backfilled or date-only historical data | Resolved with an explicit availability_basis rule. Sources without vintages use the capture rule: a value captured by the end of its observation day is available at receipt, a backfill at 23:59:59 of the observation day in the source's zone, flagged availability_estimated_backfill. Bars are available at end plus the source's publication lag. SEC facts are available at acceptance, else at the end of the filed day. Every strategy feature must declare availability_basis from BAR_CLOSE_PLUS_LAG, PUBLISHED_PLUS_ALLOWANCE, DATE_ONLY_NEXT_SESSION or CAPTURED. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/data/sources/common.py:63-67`; `backend/src/unknown/research/spec.py:63`; `backend/src/unknown/research/spec.py:278-288`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/ibkr/history.py:439-440`; `backend/src/unknown/data/sec.py:75-82` |
| BR-DAT-07 | high | omission | The 'untouched' holdout has no access control, reuse limit or consumption rule, so repeated agent candidates overfit it | Resolved. The holdout belongs to a pre-registered experiment and is consumed at most once, by the OWNER or the SYSTEM evaluator, never an AGENT. Every attempt, granted or refused, is logged. A dataset view reaching into the holdout needs a grant issued by the registry (a hand-made grant is refused); a non-holdout trial whose manifest reaches the holdout is refused, and leaked returns fail the trial, which still counts. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/research/registry.py:599-637`; `backend/src/unknown/research/bars.py:172-244` |
| BR-LED-02 | high | omission | Residual equity of stopped, below-target or risk-stopped sessions has no destination bucket, and the reference deadlocks permanently after RISK_STOP | ADR-0008: an unsuccessful session (BELOW_TARGET, OWNER_ENDED, EXPIRED) ends CLOSED once flat, fee-final and reconciled. Its cash returns to `unallocated` and its receivables are re-owned to `unallocated` but stay non-available until settlement confirms them; no successor is created. A session risk trigger suspends the session (reductions still allowed) and the owner can end it, so nothing deadlocks. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/sessions.py:365-420`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/sessions.py:423-441`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/sessions.py:478-485`; `docs/adr/0008-session-state-machine.md#Closing an unsuccessful session` |
| BR-LED-03 | high | omission | No rule for fee adjustments, corrections or busts that arrive after completion and protection, and 'sufficiently finalized' fees is undefined | 'Fees final' is defined: completion needs a broker commission or an owner-accepted estimate for every session execution. Whatever arrives after a session ended is booked to whoever holds its balances now (protected:<id> after completion, unallocated after close; ADR-0008 amendment), and the completion and successor stand. A late fee posts there with a case (blocking if that owner's cash would go negative). A late fill, a bust (a correction to quantity zero, which used to fail the whole command and discard the broker fact) or a price correction is booked there and opens one blocking POST_COMPLETION_ADJUSTMENT per execution; a bust of a buy whose shares were already sold restates the sale's cost there; a cash shortfall moves to exception:<case>. Inventory left in protected:<id> or unallocated is sold by an owner DISPOSAL intent that reserves and books only that owner's lots, runs the full pretrade (the owner's disposal stands in for the session-state check, as the decision records) and reconciles like any trade; a bust, correction or fee change of a disposal fill opens a case too. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/sessions.py:382-384`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:44-53`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:490-495`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:533-554`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:763-807`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:93-100`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:174-183`; `docs/adr/0008-session-state-machine.md#Owner disposal` |
| BR-LED-05 | high | omission | The execution table has no provider revision or group key, so a related-identifier correction is stored as a second independent fill (double count) unless an unspecified rule sets correction_of | Executions are keyed by a provider-scoped exec_key. A correction carries correction_of (the IBKR adapter derives it from the execId revision suffix and links to the latest prior revision) and is booked as reversing entries plus a replacement, never as a second fill. A re-delivery with identical economics is a no-op. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:242-283`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:653-746`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/ibkr/mapping.py:166-176`; `backend/src/unknown/store/schema.py:124-126` |
| BR-LED-06 | high | contradiction | Ch9 says the appendix schema specifies lots and fees, but it has no lot, fee, reservation, settlement-obligation, policy-bucket or successor-request tables | The core schema (not contracts/ledger_schema.sql) has lots, holds (reservations), settlements (obligations with expected date, relieved account code and owner), postings with an owner column (the policy buckets), successors (UNIQUE predecessor) and executions carrying fee_estimate and fee_final. Holds, the order and its outbox row commit in one event. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/store/schema.py:73-133`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:150-170` |
| BR-LED-07 | high | contradiction | Money and price columns have no scale or currency, and the scale sits on a single mutable instrument row | Every amount and price is stored as canonical decimal text that carries its own scale (ADR-0004), postings and orders carry their currency, and a journal amount must be exact at its currency's ledger scale. | BR-LED-08 | high | omission | Buys have no unsettled payable, so ledger 'settled cash' falls on trade date and cannot be reconciled exactly against broker settled cash | A buy credits PAYABLE on trade date (ADR-0006) and availability subtracts payables at once. Settlement moves PAYABLE to CASH only when broker settled cash agrees exactly, and reconciliation compares ledger CASH plus due settlements with broker settled cash, reporting unsettled differences separately. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:339-347`; `backend/src/unknown/core/ledger.py:158-182`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:148-199`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:268-287` |
| BR-LED-09 | high | omission | Deposits, withdrawals, interest, account-level charges and external trades have no bucket assignment or authorization rule | ADR-0006 'External activity'. Any unexplained broker cash change is a blocking CASH_MISMATCH. The owner attributes deposits, withdrawals, interest or charges to `outside` with record_external_cash_flow; a withdrawal larger than outside cash is refused and stays blocking. Only authorize_capital moves value from outside to unallocated. Manual trades are owned by `external` with their own lots and funded from `outside`, otherwise a blocking OVERCOMMITTED case. | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/control.py:174-186`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/admin.py:196-231`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/broker.py:566-641`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/recon.py:190-194`; `docs/adr/0006-ledger-owner-dimension.md#External activity` |
| BR-LED-11 | high | ambiguity | The fee envelope's composition, size and release time are undefined, and the reference releases it on fill or cancel before fees are final | The envelope is the policy fee model's uncapped per-share estimate (with its minimum) plus a margin, rounded up; the cash hold is ceil(leaves x limit x multiplier) plus the envelope not yet consumed by estimates. A stop is never blocked by persistence (ADR-0010 amendment): when the database cannot record an OWNER or SYSTEM stop of this account, the core service latches it in memory, refuses exposure-increasing commands at its socket, transmits no buy (a claimed buy is recorded REJECTED NOT_TRANSMITTED), sends a best-effort cancel straight to the broker for every open buy in the ledger and every buy the broker lists with our client order id (either source alone suffices; foreign orders are not touched), logs CRITICAL, shows the latch in health and preflight (STOP_LATCHED_IN_MEMORY) and mirrors it in an fsync'd <account>.stop-latch.json, so a restart starts latched. As soon as writes work, the stop is recorded under its own id, then a SYSTEM stop carrying the evidence (error, cancels, the ids it stood in for); only then is the memory latch released. A malformed id, a role that may not stop or another account never engages it, and an unreadable or tampered latch file fails closed with the service's own stop (RB-12). | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/control.py:18-25`; `backend/src/unknown/risk/pretrade.py:94`; `backend/src/unknown/service/core.py:150-162`; `backend/src/unknown/service/core.py:173-205`; `backend/src/unknown/service/core.py:259-322`; `backend/src/unknown/service/core.py:377-380`; `backend/src/unknown/gateway/runner.py:206-214`; `docs/adr/0010-control-plane-stop-start-recovery.md#Memory latch`; `docs/runbooks/README.md#RB-12 Red stop latched in memory` |

| BR-ORD-20 | medium | ambiguity | 'Fail closed' on lossless queue overflow is undefined for inbound broker events | Every inbound broker event is appended to a durable, hash-chained, append-only journal (<account>.events: fsync'd, with the core store's durability settings) before it is applied. At start the service applies unapplied entries before any request or new event. The in-memory hand-off is bounded: if the journal cannot be written, the runner stops taking events (at most one batch waits in memory), latches the stop (EVENT_JOURNAL_UNWRITABLE), records an INCOMPLETE reconciliation and, in pump(), fails closed before the outbox pass, so no new buy is transmitted; journaled entries are still applied. ## Residuals recorded on resolved findings

| ID | Disposition | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| BR-DAT-01 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | ADR-0017 is proposed, not accepted. The core does not consult the promotion registry: a mandate can list an artifact id whatever its research status, and the id carries 16 hex characters of the hash, not the full hash. |
| BR-DAT-02 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Exposure is the session's, not the artifact's: every principal's orders in the session count as the artifact's current exposure, so one artifact per session is the documented rule until the core attributes exposure per strategy (docs/strategy/README.md). |
| BR-DAT-03 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Full and zero fills are tested on the buy side, partial fills on both sides. Live transaction-cost analysis of real fills (CAP-OMS-13) is not built. |
| BR-DAT-04 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The risk term is a sizing rule of the backtest and the runner, not a pretrade check: an owner's manual intent is not sized by it. Schema v1 specs still run in research with the legacy rules (no risk term), the runner refuses them, and the committed FS-20261022-R2 report was produced from v1 specs and has not been regenerated. |
| BR-DAT-05 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | PUBLISHED_PLUS_ALLOWANCE and DATE_ONLY_NEXT_SESSION are declared but no ingested dataset uses them yet (no earnings dates or corporate actions are ingested); the strict parser enforces the enum, but no test submits an unknown basis. |
| BR-DAT-06 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Barring restated-only data from promotion evidence waits for the promotion gate (I5). |
| BR-DAT-08 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The evidence bundle is the promotion service's typed evidence records, not a separately versioned schema document. |
| BR-DAT-09 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Cross-market rebalancing is not exercised: the engine refuses instruments whose currency or settlement cycle differs from the run's (USD, T+1). |
| BR-DAT-10 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | There is no all-in (operating-cost) gate: all-in costs default to 0 and are labelled NOT CONFIGURED. |
| BR-DAT-12 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Version 1 (free-text evidence) is still accepted. Agent answers never carry a forecast block, because no calibration record exists, so no agent proposal is FORECAST_QUANTIFIED. No proposal feeds a strategy feature or an order. |
| BR-DAT-15 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Exposure at runtime is the session's, not the artifact's: every principal's orders in the session count as the artifact's current holdings, so one artifact per session is the documented rule (BR-DAT-02). |
| BR-DAT-17 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Upstream lineage and licence start/end are not registry fields yet. |
| BR-DAT-18 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The restart check compares the journal's latest decision only, and a mismatch blocks entries for the process's lifetime rather than until an owner acknowledges it. The backtest builds no feature rows, so on gappy real data it decides where the runner blocks. Freshness is checked by the runner (at the decision and at transmission), not by the core. |
| BR-DAT-19 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The core does not enforce the window after transmission: the runner's cancel ends it, so a runner that is down at the window's end leaves the remainder working until the close. The backtest references the session open (daily bars carry no quote), assumes uniform intraday volume and still fills on the whole day's bar: optimistic, labelled and not modelled (docs/strategy/README.md). |
| BR-DAT-20 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | US IBKR Pro schedules only (other jurisdictions are refused, not modelled); the core reservation envelope uses risk/fees.py, a separate implementation that the core replay cross-checks. |
| BR-DAT-21 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The link runs from the decision event to the order ids; an order carries only a free-form decision_ref (the strategy runner's names its decision), not a verified decision event id. |
| BR-LED-02 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | No ReviewHold owner: FAILED_REVIEW keeps the session's balances in session:<id> until the owner resolves the case (see BR-SES-05). |
| BR-LED-03 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The reviewer's COMPLETED_RESTATED flag was not adopted: a completion is never restated (ADR-0008), and the owner reviews the case instead. Neither the terminal nor the CLI offers a DISPOSAL form; the owner sends submit_intent with purpose DISPOSAL through POST /api/commands (RB-14). |
| BR-LED-05 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Same execution id with changed content is tracked in BR-ORD-05. |
| BR-LED-07 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Instrument metadata is one current row (a change needs a higher metadata_version); earlier versions survive only in the InstrumentRegistered events, and the version rule has no test. |
| BR-LED-09 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Interest and account charges have no dedicated posting yet (the INTEREST and OTHER_FEES codes are unused); they are recorded as external flows of `outside`. |
| BR-LED-11 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The paper policy's fee schedule is marked UNVERIFIED, and regulatory fees and transaction taxes are not modelled (US sell-side fees accrue against the sale receivable). The single operational_buffer and the per-order envelope are separate values. |
| BR-LED-18 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Strategy artifact hash and decision evidence are a strategy_id and a free-form decision_ref (BR-DAT-21). |
| BR-OPS-01 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Credential rotation for an unreachable or terminated old host is not covered. |
| BR-OPS-02 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Orders created after the backup are unknown to the restored ledger, so reconciliation treats them as foreign activity (latch) instead of re-attributing them to sessions; there is no continuous off-host journal shipping (RPO undefined). |
| BR-OPS-05 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | An UNKNOWN order never escalates to a latch by itself; it blocks entries until broker evidence resolves it (BR-ORD-03). |
| BR-OPS-09 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Paid data and cloud have no budget objects because both are refused (GATE-01); the cents fields in config/paper_defaults.json and the deployment example are unused. |
| BR-OPS-10 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Evaluator and author are distinguished by principal id; separate evaluator credentials are not built. No online learning exists, so no online parameter update path exists to guard. |
| BR-ORD-02 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | A gap that persists is reported by reconciliation as a blocking EXECUTIONS_MISSING difference (recon.py:88-91); no test names that difference. |
| BR-ORD-03 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The IBKR adapter is conclusive only when placeOrder was never called, so a transmitted order with no trace always needs the owner's close. The evidence reference is recorded, not checked against a statement (statement ingestion, CAP-OMS-07, is not built). Neither the terminal nor the CLI offers the close: the owner sends it through POST /api/commands, and the terminal's acknowledge action resolves the case without closing the order (RB-13). |
| BR-ORD-05 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The reviewer's rule for a late lower or equal revision (store, do not apply) is not in the core. A lower revision that arrives after a higher one is linked by the IBKR adapter to the latest lower revision it saw, so the core reverses the higher, effective revision and books the stale one in its place, and opens no case (checked against 8b98d00 by a probe, not by a test). Only the adapter's OUT_OF_ORDER_REVISION alert (in memory, `unknown.cli alerts`; no test) and a later reconciliation difference, if the revisions differ in quantity or price, show it. There is no provider_version column; the revision is parsed from the execution id. |
| BR-ORD-06 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The ORDER_MISSING_AT_BROKER and ORDER_UNRESOLVED branches (recon.py:100-103, 124-125) have no test of their own. |
| BR-ORD-07 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | ADR-0009 keeps one state label rather than the reviewer's three orthogonal fields; a broker-held order is UNKNOWN plus a broker_held record, not a separate HELD state. The real IBKR sequences that produce Inactive have not been observed (no paper session; docs/integrations/ibkr.md section 5, item 4). |
| BR-ORD-09 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | An unsolicited broker expiry or cancel of a working order is exercised only inside the seeded randomized scenarios (the fixture broker expires DAY orders on each new day); no deterministic test isolates it. |
| BR-ORD-11 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Strategy artifact hash and instrument metadata version are not on the order (BR-DAT-21). |
| BR-ORD-12 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | No good-till cancel for a working order before the close; only wind-down orders are repriced by cancel after reprice_after_s. |
| BR-ORD-13 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The values are fixed in code rather than the versioned policy; the non-convergence branch and the order-set difference kinds have no test of their own. |
| BR-ORD-14 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Statement (Flex) backfill is not implemented; the owner attestation is the only way past a gap. |
| BR-ORD-15 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | test_f30_manual_sale_of_session_shares_is_flagged_not_netted_silently asserts the blocking case, not the refused follow-up sell; the refusal comes from the open EXECUTION_ANOMALY, which test_f36 shows blocks a reducing sell. |
| BR-ORD-16 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The refusing test uses EXECUTION_ANOMALY; no test opens a POSITION_MISMATCH and then attempts a sell. The test asserts the refusal, not its code (OPEN_EXCEPTIONS was confirmed by re-running the scenario on 2026-10-22). |
| BR-ORD-19 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | No test asserts that an ACKNOWLEDGED order keeps its state across a disconnect; broker_connectivity (broker.py:969-981) only updates the control row. |
| BR-ORD-20 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | An event the adapter has received but the gateway has not yet appended (in the adapter's own in-memory FIFO, or in the gateway's single batch) is lost at a crash and recovered only by reconciliation, inside IBKR's execution-history window (a gap needs owner acknowledgement). While the journal fails, the adapter's FIFO keeps growing in memory and the API session is not disconnected. The journal is not in the online backup and is never pruned. |
| BR-REF-02 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The fee-exceeds-proceeds test uses a single full fill; a partial fill takes the same per-execution path but is not tested separately. |
| BR-REF-13 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | A broker-initiated cancel or expiry of a working order is exercised only inside the seeded randomized scenarios (see BR-ORD-09). |
| BR-REF-17 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Truncating the newest events is not detectable without an off-host anchor; ADR-0005 #6 keeps the chain a diagnostic. |
| BR-REF-18 | RESOLVED_BY_ADR | A replay does not say whether a later control command superseded it; the terminal (in progress) must honour the duplicate flag. |
| BR-REF-19 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Any other command that fails an invariant check is rolled back and reported to its caller without latching; the next full check (a reconciliation result, process start or green start) latches LEDGER_INVARIANT. The latch stays in the control row rather than in its own append-only table; the memory latch covers a control row that cannot be written. |
| BR-REF-21 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The amended content is kept in the event log and the case, not as a second executions row, and there is no provider_version column (BR-ORD-05). |
| BR-SES-02 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The reviewer's account latch on every such event was not adopted: the blocking case blocks new exposure (pretrade and preflight), and the stop is not latched. Neither the terminal nor the CLI offers a DISPOSAL form (RB-14). |
| BR-SES-03 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | ADR-0008 returns everything to unallocated; the reviewer's split (amount above seed to protected) was not adopted. The RISK_ENDED outcome has no trigger yet: session risk triggers suspend instead. |
| BR-SES-04 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Funding does not compare the remaining allowance with a new session's loss budget; a breach blocks every entry. |
| BR-SES-05 | RESOLVED_BY_ADR | The sessions.state column has no CHECK constraint. |
| BR-SES-07 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The reviewer's freeze-at-funding proposal was not adopted; ADR-0007 governs. |
| BR-SES-08 | RESOLVED_BY_ADR | Account-level limits and eligibility are read from the active policy (orders.py:87), so a new owner-published policy changes them for running sessions in either direction; this is not stated in ADR-0007. |
| BR-SES-13 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | The memory latch lives in one core process, and its file sits on the same volume as the database: if the file cannot be written either, a restart before the database recovers starts without the stop, held only by the restart's recovery hold (RB-12). Red from the terminal needs the API and the core socket; with the core down, the broker's own cancel-all is the only path (RB-01). There is no installation-wide latch across accounts (one core per account), and the terminal shows STOP_LATCHED_IN_MEMORY only as a generic preflight blocker. |
| BR-SES-15 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | A late settlement does not latch the account (decision in ADR-0008). |
| BR-SES-16 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | ADR-0008 sends a session risk trigger to SUSPENDED; the reviewer's wind-down-on-drawdown was not adopted. The rule that give-back during WINDING_DOWN cannot trigger a drawdown (valuation.py:93-99) has no test of its own. |
| BR-SES-18 | RESOLVED_BY_ADR | There is no monotonic version number; order is the published_at/activated_at time and event sequence. |
| BR-SES-22 | RESOLVED_IN_CODE | Estimated exit fees are not deducted from the observation and one fresh evaluation suffices, so a bid-level observation can still end BELOW_TARGET (tested in BR-SES-06). |

## Evidence per finding

### BR-DAT-01: Chapter 20 lets automatic selection pick a merely 'validated' artifact, including in a running session; Chapter 10 and the status ladder require preapproval and owner action

- `docs/book/10-sessions-and-capital.md`:29: "Widening limits, raising capital or changing a running strategy's parameters requires an explicit owner action with an audit record."
- `docs/book/10-sessions-and-capital.md`:31: "Automatic promotion chooses among preapproved strategy artifacts inside a fixed envelope."
- `docs/book/10-sessions-and-capital.md`:3: "It owns an immutable seed allocation, target definition, policy snapshot, eligible strategies, loss budget and lifecycle identifier."
- `docs/book/20-promotion-and-governance.md`:3: "Automatic promotion is allowed only for artifacts that fit an owner-approved class, parameter domain, instrument set, risk budget and evaluation protocol."

There are three conflicts. (1) Approval unit: Chapter 10 limits automatic promotion to 'preapproved strategy artifacts', meaning each artifact is approved. Chapter 20 allows any artifact that 'fit[s] an owner-approved class, parameter domain…', meaning the class is approved and new artifacts inside it need no per-artifact sign-off. (2) Status ladder: Chapter 20 line 9 orders the statuses validated < paper < eligible < active. Line 27 then lets automatic selection 'choose a validated parameter artifact', which is two rungs below eligible, so the paper stage is skipped. (3) Timing: Chapter 10 says changing a running strategy's parameters needs the owner, and that a session owns its eligible strategies immutably. Chapter 20 never says the selection waits for the next session. Its deferral sentence covers only 'A policy update', and picking an artifact is not a policy update. Read literally, Chapter 20 lets an artifact that never ran in paper and has no owner signature start trading inside a session that is already running.

Verifier: All quotes verified verbatim. I strengthened the case with the status-ladder conflict (validated comes before paper and eligible), which the original did not cite. One softening: the original said the automated evaluator assigns 'validated'. The book does not say that. Chapter 20 line 29 gives the evaluator write access to evidence, and the promotion service records transitions. Chapter 10 line 31 also uses an envelope ('If any of these change beyond the envelope, it is a new approval object'), so the two chapters partly agree; the conflict is the approval unit, the status rung and the timing. ADR-0007 (STRATEGY may act only for a session whose mandate lists its artifact) already addresses point 3 in the implementation, but the book remains contradictory.

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** Resolved for the research side. Artifacts have the statuses proposed, reproducible, validated, shadow, paper, eligible, active, quarantined and retired in a hash-chained transition log. validated -> shadow needs the owner's approval or the SYSTEM inside an owner-approved, immutable shadow envelope, and in shadow the strategy runner only records decisions; paper, eligible and active need a fresh owner approval per artifact; automatic selection runs as SYSTEM only under an owner-approved, immutable selection policy (the envelope) and considers only eligible, unexpired artifacts inside it. Point (3) holds in the core: a STRATEGY principal is an artifact id `<class>@<16 hex of the artifact hash>` bound to its token, acts only for sessions whose frozen mandate lists that id, and a new default never changes a running session's list.
- Residual: ADR-0017 is proposed, not accepted. The core does not consult the promotion registry: a mandate can list an artifact id whatever its research status, and the id carries 16 hex characters of the hash, not the full hash.
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:108-123`; `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:744-792`; `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:862-880`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:56-61`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:118-119`; `backend/src/unknown/domain/principal.py:62`; `docs/adr/0017-strategy-artifacts-promotion-and-runtime.md#Artifact identity`
- Tests: `test_research_promotion::test_automatic_selection_only_inside_the_owner_approved_policy`; `test_research_promotion::test_eligible_and_active_need_the_owner`; `test_research_promotion::test_skipping_rungs_is_refused`; `test_research_shadow::test_shadow_sits_between_validated_and_paper`; `test_research_shadow::test_automatic_promotion_inside_the_envelope_is_recorded_with_its_evidence_and_envelope`; `test_research_shadow::test_shadow_status_permits_shadow_mode_only`; `test_desk_commands::test_strategy_permission_follows_the_session_mandate_not_the_active_default`; `test_strategy_decisions::test_issued_token_is_bound_to_its_artifact`

### BR-DAT-02: A15 never defines which order states count as 'pending exposure', and decisions have no deterministic command identity, so a decision can be duplicated

- `docs/book/15-trend-and-cross-sectional-strategies.md`:45: "map score to desired portfolio exposure; subtract current and pending exposure; apply no-trade and minimum-size rules; create a typed intent with expiry; submit to the common risk pipeline."
- `docs/book/32-formula-algorithm-and-term-index.md`:45: "**A15, trend decision:** update causal features, calculate approved score, map exposure, account for current/pending positions and create expiring intent."
- `docs/book/13-order-execution.md`:7: "Use local states `CREATED`, `RESERVED`, `SUBMIT_PENDING`, `ACKNOWLEDGED`, `PARTIALLY_FILLED`, `CANCEL_PENDING`, `FILLED`, `CANCELED`, `REJECTED`, `EXPIRED` and `UNKNOWN`."
- `docs/book/13-order-execution.md`:9: "Every intent has a globally unique client identifier and immutable payload hash."

A15 subtracts 'pending exposure' but no chapter defines which states count or at what quantity. If a CANCEL_PENDING buy with remaining quantity, or an order in SUBMIT_PENDING, is left out, the next decision cycle sends a second buy while the first can still fill. Reservations keep the second buy funded, but exposure to that name doubles. The pretrade concentration and aggregate-exposure checks (Chapter 13 line 13) also say nothing about pending orders. Identity is the second gap. Chapter 13 requires a globally unique client ID per intent but never derives it from the decision. A restart that reprocesses the same completed bar therefore creates a fresh command ID, and the Chapter 8 idempotence check (same ID, same payload) never fires. The Chapter 26 latch covers UNKNOWN only, and it sits in an ops runbook, not in the strategy contract.

Verifier: Quotes verified. Grep confirms the book never defines pending exposure or ties a command ID to a decision. Severity lowered from critical to high. ADR-0009 already blocks new entries on any UNKNOWN order and keeps holds through CANCEL_PENDING. Under ADR-0009's cash-only, limit-only eligibility, the duplicate buy stays funded and bounded by the session seed, so the residual harm is concentration and duplicated intent, not unfunded exposure. The gaps in the pending-quantity definition and the decision-derived identity are real.

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** Pending is defined once: the leaves of every order in a core open state (RESERVED, SUBMIT_PENDING, ACKNOWLEDGED, PARTIALLY_FILLED, CANCEL_PENDING, UNKNOWN), whoever placed it in the session. A pending buy counts as if it fills and a pending sell as already sold; terminal orders count nothing. The core's CONCENTRATION check adds the session's open buys in the instrument and GROSS_EXPOSURE every open buy of the account, each tested by a rejection against a control. The runner uses the same definition, emits no new intent for an instrument with an UNKNOWN or CANCEL_PENDING order in the session (PENDING_UNRESOLVED), allows at most one live intent per session, artifact and instrument (OPEN_INTENT), and checks both at the decision and again at transmission. Every command id is derived deterministically from (artifact, session date, instrument), so reprocessing a bar returns the original result. An UNKNOWN order also blocks new buys account-wide.
- Residual: Exposure is the session's, not the artifact's: every principal's orders in the session count as the artifact's current exposure, so one artifact per session is the documented rule until the core attributes exposure per strategy (docs/strategy/README.md).
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/core/order_machine.py:33`; `backend/src/unknown/core/handlers/orders.py:108-113`; `backend/src/unknown/risk/pretrade.py:153-157`; `backend/src/unknown/strategy/runner.py:252-269`; `backend/src/unknown/strategy/coreview.py:48-49`; `docs/strategy/README.md#Pending exposure (BR-DAT-02)`
- Tests: `test_strategy_pending_exposure::test_the_definition_is_the_cores_open_states`; `test_strategy_pending_exposure::test_concentration_counts_pending_buys_of_every_open_state`; `test_strategy_pending_exposure::test_gross_exposure_counts_every_open_buy`; `test_strategy_pending_exposure::test_gates`; `test_strategy_pending_exposure::test_pending_counts_in_the_runners_exposure_and_the_cores_concentration_check`; `test_strategy_pending_exposure::test_no_new_intent_beside_a_cancel_pending_order`; `test_strategy_transmission_gates::test_no_intent_is_transmitted_beside_an_order_that_became_unresolved_after_the_decision`; `test_strategy_runner::test_ids_are_deterministic_valid_and_distinct`; `test_orders_lifecycle::test_timeout_after_accept_is_unknown_blocks_entries_and_reconcile_resolves`

### BR-DAT-03: Eq. 7.1 implementation shortfall: the reported execution component is wrong whenever an order partially fills

- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:21: "IS=s\left(\sum_j q_jp_j-Qp_0\right)+fees+opportunity\ cost"
- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:23: "If some quantity is unfilled, define its opportunity-cost mark and report it separately."
- `docs/book/18-market-making-and-optimal-execution.md`:37: "Compare schedules on implementation shortfall, residual exposure, completion rate and tail cost under the same simulated market paths."

The execution term subtracts Q·p0 for the whole parent quantity, but only Σq_j shares executed. I recomputed the example: buy Q=100 at p0=$10.00, 50 shares fill at $10.10, fees are $1, and the unfilled 50 are marked at $10.50. The correct IS is 5 + 25 + 1 = $31. The book's execution term alone is 505 − 1000 = −$495. With the conventional opportunity cost (Q−Σq)(p_mark−p0) = $25, Eq. 7.1 totals −$469. For a sell with fills at $9.90, the execution term becomes +$505, a large spurious cost. The total comes out right only if 'opportunity cost' is read as s·(Q−Σq)·p_mark, the full mark value of the unfilled shares, which is not a cost. Even under that reading, the execution component that line 23 says to report separately is still −$495. Schedules that seldom fill buys would therefore rank best in Chapter 18 comparisons and in promotion evidence.

Verifier: Quote and arithmetic verified in scratch Python: −495 execution term, −469 conventional total, 31 correct; sell execution term +505. I added the one reading under which the total is correct (opportunity cost defined as the unfilled shares' full mark value). Under that reading the separately reported execution component is still wrong, so the error stands.

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** Resolved in the research engine: implementation_shortfall computes the corrected eq. 7.1, IS = s(sum q_j p_j - (sum q_j) p0) + fees + s(Q - sum q_j)(p_mark - p0), with execution, fee and opportunity components in exact decimals plus bps, the mark being the close of the execution session; every finished order records it.
- Residual: Full and zero fills are tested on the buy side, partial fills on both sides. Live transaction-cost analysis of real fills (CAP-OMS-13) is not built.
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/research/engine.py:397-410`; `backend/src/unknown/research/engine.py:595-596`; `backend/src/unknown/research/common.py:91`
- Tests: `test_research_engine::test_br_dat_03_known_answers`

### BR-DAT-04: Eq. 6.4 sizing leaves fixed and minimum fees out of the risk term; the worked example permits a trade whose loss at the stop is 3.5x its budget

- `docs/book/06-portfolio-and-risk.md`:35: "\frac{B_{risk}}{d+c},\frac{C_{available}-f}{p_{limit}}"
- `docs/book/06-portfolio-and-risk.md`:37: "Here $d$ is an adverse price distance per share, $c$ a conservative per-share cost allowance, $f$ a fixed fee allowance and $B_{risk}$ the planned loss budget."
- `docs/book/06-portfolio-and-risk.md`:39: "Example: a $100 session with a $0.50 planned-risk allowance, a $0.20 distance and $0.05 cost allowance permits two shares by the risk formula. A $60 share price permits only one by cash. A $0.75 minimum commission might make the entire strategy economically unsuitable."

The example's arithmetic is right: floor(0.50/0.25) = 2 and floor((100−f)/60) = 1. The formula is the problem. The fixed or minimum fee f appears only in the cash term, so the risk term ignores per-order charges. With the book's own numbers and a $0.75 minimum commission on entry and exit, the 1 share the formula permits loses 0.25 + 1.50 = $1.75 at the stop, which is 3.5x the $0.50 budget. If cash allowed 2 shares, the loss would be $2.00 (4x), which equals the whole 2% session drawdown trigger (paper_defaults: 200 bps of a $100 seed). The text only says the commission 'might' make the strategy unsuitable, when the sizer should output zero. The cash term also subtracts only the fixed allowance f, not per-share variable fees, so it can disagree with the Chapter 9 reservation (limit × quantity + fee envelope). The order then fails at reservation instead of being sized correctly.

Verifier: Quotes verified and arithmetic recomputed. I corrected the original: the cash term binds in the worked example, so q = 1 and the loss is $1.75 (3.5x). The 4x / $2.00 figure applies only if cash allowed 2 shares. I also removed the original's claim that the cash term disagrees with Chapter 9 on the exit fee: the Chapter 9 reservation covers only the entry fee envelope.

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** research.sizing.plan_orders is the one A15 sizer, used by the backtest and the strategy runner. Its buy step limits the position to the largest whole Q with Q x (d + c) + Fee_entry + Fee_exit <= B_risk. Both fees come from risk.fees.envelope, the function the core reserves fees with (minimum per order included), under the active policy's fee model at runtime and the engine's schedule in the backtest; zero means no order. The cash term then sizes the buy to what the funding budget can reserve including the fee envelope. Spec schema v2 declares rules.sizing.risk_budget. The book's example sizes to 0 with a 0.75 minimum each way; against a real core, the 100.00 fixture session buys 1 share of a 10.00 instrument instead of 4, and none of a 42.00 one.
- Residual: The risk term is a sizing rule of the backtest and the runner, not a pretrade check: an owner's manual intent is not sized by it. Schema v1 specs still run in research with the legacy rules (no risk term), the runner refuses them, and the committed FS-20261022-R2 report was produced from v1 specs and has not been regenerated.
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/research/sizing.py:131-157`; `backend/src/unknown/research/sizing.py:158-195`; `backend/src/unknown/research/spec.py:323-329`; `docs/strategy/README.md#Risk term (BR-DAT-04)`
- Tests: `test_research_risk_sizing::test_the_minimum_commission_sizes_the_trade_to_zero`; `test_research_risk_sizing::test_the_risk_term_prices_fees_with_the_cores_envelope_function`; `test_research_risk_sizing::test_one_sizing_function_serves_the_backtest_and_the_runner`; `test_research_risk_sizing::test_a_small_account_buys_nothing_when_fees_exceed_the_risk_budget`; `test_research_risk_sizing::test_a_small_account_is_sized_to_zero_by_fees_in_the_backtest`; `test_strategy_risk_window::test_fees_in_the_risk_term_size_buys_down_and_to_zero`

### BR-DAT-05: No rule for assigning availability time to backfilled or date-only historical data

- `docs/book/03-instruments-and-time.md`:47: "The feature's availability time is the earliest time the system could actually use it. A filing published at 14:00 but received at 14:03 cannot influence a simulated 14:01 decision."
- `docs/book/11-data-platform.md`:29: "Ingest the earliest licensed timestamp, publication timestamp, revisions and provider identifier."
- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:29: "Historical data acquired today can still contain future knowledge relative to a historical decision."
- `docs/book/11-data-platform.md`:41: "A dataset becomes eligible when a replay can reconstruct its availability"

Availability is defined by when 'the system could actually use it', which is a receipt time. Nearly all research history is backfilled and has no local receipt. Many sources carry only a date: daily bars, fundamentals keyed by filing or period date, earnings dates without before-open or after-close timing, and corporate-action announcement dates. EDGAR's filing date can differ from its acceptance timestamp. One engineer will stamp the publication date at 00:00, or the publication timestamp with zero latency, and so leak. Another will add a latency allowance or roll to the next session. Backtest P&L and promotion decisions then differ. This is the main leakage path the point-in-time store must close, and Chapter 11 makes dataset eligibility depend on it without saying how.

Verifier: Quotes verified. Grep finds no rule for receipt-less or date-only data anywhere in the book. This also covers which host's capture is canonical (the reason I refuted the separate Mac/AWS ingestion-writer issue).

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** Resolved with an explicit availability_basis rule. Sources without vintages use the capture rule: a value captured by the end of its observation day is available at receipt, a backfill at 23:59:59 of the observation day in the source's zone, flagged availability_estimated_backfill. Bars are available at end plus the source's publication lag. SEC facts are available at acceptance, else at the end of the filed day. Every strategy feature must declare availability_basis from BAR_CLOSE_PLUS_LAG, PUBLISHED_PLUS_ALLOWANCE, DATE_ONLY_NEXT_SESSION or CAPTURED.
- Residual: PUBLISHED_PLUS_ALLOWANCE and DATE_ONLY_NEXT_SESSION are declared but no ingested dataset uses them yet (no earnings dates or corporate actions are ingested); the strict parser enforces the enum, but no test submits an unknown basis.
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/data/sources/common.py:63-67`; `backend/src/unknown/research/spec.py:63`; `backend/src/unknown/research/spec.py:278-288`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/ibkr/history.py:439-440`; `backend/src/unknown/data/sec.py:75-82`
- Tests: `test_sources_treasury::test_capture_rule_bounds`; `test_sources_treasury::test_live_capture_and_availability`; `test_sources_ecb::test_exact_values_and_availability`; `test_ibkr_marketdata::test_intraday_epoch_bar_end_availability_and_completeness`; `test_research_engine::test_a_late_publication_is_invisible_until_available`; `test_data_sec::test_acceptance_time_parsing`

### BR-DAT-06: The point-in-time store has no knowledge-time or supersedes fields, and no rule for datasets that lack vintages

- `docs/book/11-data-platform.md`:13: "Corrections create new versions; they do not silently alter a historical experiment."
- `docs/book/11-data-platform.md`:17: "The feature contract includes entity, event time, availability time, feature version, value, unit, missingness reason and provenance."
- `docs/book/11-data-platform.md`:35: "Estimate revisions require point-in-time vendor snapshots."
- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:29: "A revised macro series should use its historical vintage."

The book states the as-of principle: Chapter 3 line 47, Chapter 7 line 29, and Chapter 11 line 35 ('Trailing figures must use reports available at the decision time'). It gives no data model to implement it. The feature contract has no recorded or knowledge time and no supersedes link, so the store cannot answer 'value as known at T' for a corrected or restated key. Line 13 also leaves open whether a correction becomes a new dataset version (system time) or a new row available at the correction's own publication time. The book also never says what to do with a dataset bought today that has only restated values and no vintages, which is common for fundamentals. One engineer will use it, with leakage; another will bar it.

Verifier: Quotes verified. I narrowed the claim and lowered severity from high to medium. The original said the book never states what a backtest should see, which is wrong: it does state the as-of principle in three places. What is missing is the bitemporal fields and the rule for restated-only datasets.

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** ADR-0015: the observation table is bitemporal (available_time plus known_from/known_to and supersedes); a restatement is a later publication, a silent correction closes the previous version's knowledge interval, and as_of(t) never sees a later publication. Datasets declare vintage_support; one without vintages is reconstructed only from what this system captured.
- Residual: Barring restated-only data from promotion evidence waits for the promotion gate (I5).
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/data/pit.py:47-58`; `backend/src/unknown/data/pit.py:164-235`; `backend/src/unknown/data/registry.py:41`; `docs/adr/0015-data-platform-point-in-time-store.md#Point-in-time read`
- Tests: `test_pit_store::test_restatement_never_leaks_backwards`; `test_pit_store::test_silent_correction_of_same_publication`; `test_pit_store::test_missing_is_not_zero`; `test_data_sec::test_ingest_point_in_time`

### BR-DAT-07: The 'untouched' holdout has no access control, reuse limit or consumption rule, so repeated agent candidates overfit it

- `docs/book/04-probability-and-estimation.md`:48: "An untouched holdout must remain untouched after a disappointing result."
- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:27: "Use chronological training, validation and untouched test periods. In walk-forward evaluation, train only on past data, choose parameters on an inner validation period, and evaluate on the next unseen segment."
- `docs/book/01-product-contract.md`:11: "| R03 | Rigorous quantitative foundations | Research laboratory | Reproducible experiments and untouched evaluation |"
- `docs/book/20-promotion-and-governance.md`:17: "Maintain a complete trial register so that repeated candidate generation is visible."

Agents generate candidates autonomously, and Chapter 20 allows automatic promotion. If every candidate in a family is scored on the same 'untouched' test period and the best is promoted, that period has become a validation set. The trial register makes the repetition visible but does not stop it. Nothing defines who may read holdout results, how many evaluations a holdout allows, whether walk-forward outer segments count as untouched, or what replaces a holdout once used. Two implementations would give very different false-promotion rates.

Verifier: Quotes verified. Grep for holdout, held-out and untouched finds no access, reuse or consumption rule anywhere in the book.

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** Resolved. The holdout belongs to a pre-registered experiment and is consumed at most once, by the OWNER or the SYSTEM evaluator, never an AGENT. Every attempt, granted or refused, is logged. A dataset view reaching into the holdout needs a grant issued by the registry (a hand-made grant is refused); a non-holdout trial whose manifest reaches the holdout is refused, and leaked returns fail the trial, which still counts.
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/research/registry.py:599-637`; `backend/src/unknown/research/bars.py:172-244`
- Tests: `test_research_registry::test_holdout_is_consumed_once_and_every_attempt_is_logged`; `test_research_registry::test_agents_never_read_the_holdout`; `test_research_registry::test_a_hand_made_grant_does_not_open_the_holdout`; `test_research_registry::test_a_non_holdout_trial_whose_manifest_reaches_the_holdout_is_refused`; `test_research_registry::test_holdout_returns_from_a_development_trial_are_refused_and_the_trial_still_counts`; `test_research_study::test_holdout_was_consumed_exactly_once_and_cannot_be_reused`

### BR-DAT-08: No canonical strategy-spec and evidence schema: four partial field lists, none matching requirement RES-01

- `docs/book/20-promotion-and-governance.md`:7: "Its registry entry includes author, training interval, evaluation manifest, allowed markets, expected horizon, capacity estimate, expiration and rollback predecessor."
- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:45: "The experiment artifact contains hypothesis, manifest, search history, fold results, uncertainty, stress results, rejected alternatives and a signed promotion recommendation."
- `docs/book/15-trend-and-cross-sectional-strategies.md`:3: "Each candidate needs an information set, signal equation, position rule, execution rule, cost model, invalidation condition and evidence manifest."
- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:3: "Start every experiment with a hypothesis, universe, information clock, execution rule, cost model, benchmark, parameter-search budget and rejection criterion."

The repository's requirement RES-01 (docs/requirements/register.md line 123) asks for a registry with hypothesis, equations, variables and units, causal features, rules, costs, capacity, risk limits, failure conditions, splits, baselines, sensitivity, stress and promotion evidence. The book spreads this over four lists that do not agree. The Chapter 20 registry entry, the record the promotion service checks, has no hypothesis, equations, entry/exit/sizing rules, cost model, risk limits, validation or holdout intervals, or baselines. The Chapter 7 artifact has no capacity, allowed markets or expiry. The rules-only ablation that R04 requires (Chapter 1 line 12) appears in none of them. Schemas built from different chapters will disagree on what a promotable artifact must carry.

Verifier: Quotes verified, and the RES-01 text was found in docs/requirements/register.md. I corrected two overstatements and lowered severity from high to medium. Chapter 7 line 45 does name the list whose absence makes a candidate 'incomplete'. Chapter 20 lines 15 and 17 independently require stress outcomes and robustness to parameter perturbation, so a promotion service following Chapter 20 would not pass a candidate with no stress or sensitivity evidence. What remains is that no single schema exists.

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** Resolved. StrategySpec is one strict, hashed, versioned schema holding every RES-01 field (hypothesis, equations, variables with units, causal features, data requirements, rules, costs, capacity, risk limits, validation plan, baselines, sensitivity and stress plans, required promotion evidence); unknown, missing or out-of-range fields are refused. Promotion evidence is typed by kind (REPLAY_EXACT, CORE_REPLAY_MATCH, WALK_FORWARD_OOS, PAIRED_BOOTSTRAP, HOLDOUT, COST_SENSITIVITY, STRESS, ...) and a transition without the required evidence is refused.
- Residual: The evidence bundle is the promotion service's typed evidence records, not a separately versioned schema document.
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/research/spec.py:63-67`; `backend/src/unknown/research/spec.py:278-288`; `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:108-123`; `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:521-562`
- Tests: `test_research_spec::test_every_res01_field_is_present_and_required`; `test_research_spec::test_builders_produce_valid_specs_with_stable_hashes`; `test_research_spec::test_required_promotion_evidence`; `test_research_spec::test_unknown_field_rejected`; `test_research_promotion::test_missing_evidence_refuses`

### BR-DAT-09: Bar interval convention and cross-market 'rebalance date' alignment are undefined

- `docs/book/05-time-series-and-features.md`:9: "Record the bar construction version and how incomplete bars are treated. A strategy cannot use the final high or close of a bar before the bar is complete."
- `docs/book/15-trend-and-cross-sectional-strategies.md`:19: "At rebalance date $t$, define a past-return score over a documented interval, optionally skipping the most recent period:"
- `docs/book/03-instruments-and-time.md`:49: "Use timezone-aware calendars for display and market rules, UTC for storage, and immutable calendar versions for replay."

The book never says whether a bar's timestamp is its start or its end, and never gives a bar record its own availability time. Eq. 15.2 and Chapter 15 line 23 ('Rank only instruments eligible at that date') define cross-sectional momentum at a rebalance 'date'. For a US, India and EU universe, a date-keyed join at an Indian or EU decision instant picks up the same date's US close, which is still in the future. Chapter 7 line 7 ('only data whose availability time precedes its decision') would prevent this if bars carried an availability time, but the bar definition never connects to that rule.

Verifier: Quotes verified. Grep finds no bar start/end convention. Severity lowered from high to medium: Chapter 7 line 7's availability rule already forbids the leak if applied to bars. The gap is the bar record fields and the per-market meaning of 'date' in Eq. 15.2.

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** Resolved. Bars are stored as [start, end) with an availability time (end plus the source's publication lag), and corrections are later versions. Every read is 'latest version with availability time <= decision instant' at a UTC decision instant, never a calendar-date join; the research bar series and the point-in-time store agree at every availability boundary. IBKR historical bars follow the same rule.
- Residual: Cross-market rebalancing is not exercised: the engine refuses instruments whose currency or settlement cycle differs from the run's (USD, T+1).
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/research/bars.py:32-62`; `backend/src/unknown/research/bars.py:106-115`; `backend/src/unknown/broker/ibkr/history.py:439-448`
- Tests: `test_research_engine::test_decisions_see_only_bars_available_at_the_decision_instant`; `test_research_engine::test_a_correction_is_invisible_before_its_publication_and_flags_a_revision_after`; `test_research_engine::test_bar_series_as_of_equals_research_store_as_of`; `test_ibkr_marketdata::test_no_bar_is_visible_before_its_availability_time`

### BR-DAT-10: Promotion-gate parameters have no home or unset behaviour, and the gate utility does not say whether it is net of trading costs or all-in

- `docs/book/20-promotion-and-governance.md`:15: "One example gate requires the lower bound of a paired block-bootstrap improvement interval to exceed a positive economic hurdle, with sufficient effective sample size and acceptable stress outcomes. The confidence level, block method, hurdle and trial correction must be fixed before evaluation."
- `docs/book/04-probability-and-estimation.md`:50: "The project promotion protocol uses these principles without claiming a universal sample-count threshold."
- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:15: "Model research/cloud costs separately and then report an all-in economic result as well."
- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:33: "The sample Sharpe statistic is $\bar r/s_r$ per observation."

Chapter 20 requires the gate parameters to be fixed in advance but gives them no home: they are not in the policy contents, the registry entry or the deployment record. It also does not say that automatic promotion is disabled while they are unset. The fail-closed principles in Chapter 1 line 43 and Chapter 6 line 49 cover only the deployment record and capital caps. 'Sufficient' n_eff and 'acceptable' stress have no field. The paired utility is 'net return' (Chapter 20 line 13), net of fees per Chapter 4 line 50, but Chapter 7 says a broker-positive strategy can lose money all-in, and nothing says which of the two the gate uses. Drawdown penalties are path-dependent, and the book does not say whether utility is computed per block or per resampled path. Sharpe is defined on raw per-observation returns, with 'show the convention' as the only rule.

Verifier: Quotes verified. Narrowed and lowered from high to medium. The book deliberately leaves threshold values to the owner ('example protocol'), and it already names cash and passive exposure as baselines (Chapter 7 line 35), which answers the original's 'no incumbent' question. The real gaps are where the parameters live, fail-closed behaviour when they are unset, and trading-net versus all-in.

**Disposition: RESOLVED_IN_CODE.** Resolved. The promotion protocol is an owner-approved object with exactly these fields: confidence, replications, mean block, statistic, utility coefficients, hurdle per period (net of trading costs), min n_eff, min OOS observations, min OOS Sharpe, max PBO, min DSR, required cost multiplier, max stress drawdown, holdout minimum difference, min paper days and seed. A missing or extra field refuses the protocol, so no gate can run unset; gate verdicts are recomputed under it, and evidence fails if the protocol was approved after the evaluation started.
- Residual: There is no all-in (operating-cost) gate: all-in costs default to 0 and are labelled NOT CONFIGURED.
- Where: `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:152-159`; `backend/src/unknown/research/promotion.py:168-199`
- Tests: `test_research_promotion::test_protocol_is_strict`; `test_research_promotion::test_failed_gate_refuses_validation_and_leaves_status_unchanged`; `test_research_promotion::test_protocol_approved_after_the_evaluation_started_fails_the_evidence`

### BR-DAT-11: Corporate-action handling in research is undefined: 'causally adjusted', 'corporate-action version', delisting exits, and actions between decision and transmission

- `docs/book/05-time-series-and-features.md`:7: "Use total-return prices when the research question includes distributions. Use unadjusted execution prices for order and ledger reconstruction."
- `docs/book/15-trend-and-cross-sectional-strategies.md`:7: "Let $r_t=\log(P_t/P_{t-1})$ on causally adjusted observations."
- `docs/book/15-trend-and-cross-sectional-strategies.md`:45: "validate source freshness and corporate-action version"
- `docs/book/07-research-and-backtesting.md`:29: "Include delisted instruments and actual filing availability."

The book never defines 'causally adjusted' or 'corporate-action version'. Vendor back-adjusted series apply factors from later actions. Return ratios survive that, but price levels do not, so minimum-price universe filters, breakout levels compared with live quotes, and lot-affordability checks all leak. Delisted names must be included, but nothing says how a held position exits: delisting proceeds, last trade, or dropped. Dropping it reintroduces survivorship bias. Total-return series do not say whether dividends are gross or net of the owner's withholding. A split taking effect between decision (after the close) and next-session transmission leaves the intent's quantity and limit in pre-split units, because A15 checks the corporate-action version only at decision time.

Verifier: Quotes verified. Severity lowered from high to medium. I checked numerically that a uniform future back-adjustment factor leaves the TREND_DAILY_V1 score (difference of log-price EMAs) and log-return volatility unchanged (max difference 1e-15), so the first strategy's signal is unaffected. Leakage is confined to level-based features and filters, delisting exits and the split window. For buys, the Chapter 13 price collar would partly catch a pre-split limit.
